\displaystyle\langle\Delta,M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta\rangle=4\;Dh^{2}\;\Lambda_{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+R(\Delta)

with the coefficient
\Lambda_{2}
in (
3.22
), and

\displaystyle|R(\Delta)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(d)

Assume additionally that
|\cdot|V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then, there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
, any
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
we have

\displaystyle\|M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta\|_{{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,h^{2}\,|T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}|^{2}\,\bigl(\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\,|\cdot|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Remark 4.18
.

Parts (a) and (c) of the above proposition are needed for the proof of Theorem
3.6
, part (b) is needed for the proof of Proposition
3.7
, and part (d) for the proof of Proposition
3.2
.

4.5.4.2
The operator
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}
.

The kinetic term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional in (
1.18
) is contained in
\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}\Delta\rangle
with
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}
defined in (
4.121
). The following proposition allows us to extract this term.

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
be a radial function, let
A\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). We then have

\displaystyle\langle\Delta,M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}},\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}\Delta\rangle=-4\;\Lambda_{0}\;\|\Pi_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}\Psi\|_{2}^{2}

(4.123)

with
\Lambda_{0}
in (
3.20
). Moreover, for any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}},\mathbf{A}}^{(2)}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;|T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}|\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.124)

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of
[
16
, Proposition 4.13]
with the obvious replacements, and is therefore omitted.
∎

4.5.4.3
The operator
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}
.

The term
\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}\Delta\rangle
with
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}
in (
4.122
) does not contribute to the Ginzburg–Landau energy. To obtain a bound for it, we need, as in
[
16
]
, the
H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
-norm of
\Psi
.

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let
A\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume that
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}\Delta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Before we give the proof of Proposition
4.20
, we state and prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.21
.

Assume that
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}+A
with a periodic function
A\in W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

(a)

For
\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle|Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;|Z|^{4}\;\bigl[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+\varepsilon\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}+\,|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}\,\bigl(|\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})|^{2}+|\nabla(\curl\mathbf{A})|^{2}\bigr)\bigr].

(4.125)

The gradient in the last term is understood to act on each component of
\curl A
separately. The result is a vector field with nine components.

(b)

Assume that
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
. There is a constant
h_{0}>0
such that for any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\Psi,\,|Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}|^{4}\,\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;|Z|^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Proof.

We first give the proof of part (a) and start by noting that

\displaystyle\bigl[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]=-\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\varepsilon_{ijk}\;(\curl\mathbf{A})_{k}

(4.126)

with the Levi–Civita symbol
\varepsilon_{ijk}
, which is defined as
1
if
(i,j,k)
is a cyclic permutation of
\{1,2,3\}
, as
-1
if it is an anticyclic permutation, and zero if at least two indices coincide. We claim that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+2\,|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}-\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}.

(4.127)

If this holds, then we can use the fact that all terms in the above equation except for the last are self-adjoint, to see that

\displaystyle\bigl[\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}),\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr]=0.

(4.128)

To prove (
4.127
), we first note that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}+2\sum_{i=1}^{3}[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}]\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigl[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}]\bigr].

(4.129)

Moreover, an application of (
4.126
) shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}]\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}=\mathrm{i}(\curl\mathbf{A})\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}

(4.130)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigl[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}]\bigr]=-\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}).

(4.131)

We combine (
4.129
)-(
4.131
) and find

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+2\mathrm{i}\bigl((\curl\mathbf{A})\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr)\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}-\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}

Using additionally the identity
\mathrm{i}((\curl\mathbf{A})\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}=|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}
, we conclude that (
4.127
) holds.

Our next goal is to prove the formula

\displaystyle(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})^{2}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}+(Z\cdot\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}))\;(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+(Z\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\cdot\bigl((Z\cdot\nabla)(\curl\mathbf{A})\bigr).

(4.132)

We note that

\displaystyle(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})=\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}Z_{i}Z_{j}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}

(4.133)

and

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}=\sum_{m=1}^{3}\bigl(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}+\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}]+[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}]\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr).

The sum in (
4.133
) is left unchanged when we exchange the indices
i
and
j
. Motivated by this, we combine
\frac{1}{2}
times the original term and
\frac{1}{2}
times the term with
i
and
j
interchanged and find

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}+\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{3}\bigl(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}+\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt+\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]+\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\bigr]\bigr).

(4.134)

Using the commutator identity
[A,BC]=B\,[A,C]+[A,B]\,C
we write the third term as

\displaystyle\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]+\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}\bigr]\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}

(4.135)

and likewise for the term with
i
and
j
interchanged.
We also use (
4.131
) to see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}Z_{i}Z_{j}\Bigl(\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr]\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}+\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr]\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\Bigr)=(Z\cdot\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}))\;(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})

holds. Concerning the first term on the right side of (
4.135
), (
4.126
) can be used to show

\displaystyle\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(m)}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\varepsilon_{imk}\,\partial_{j}(\curl\mathbf{A})_{k}),

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}Z_{i}Z_{j}\bigl(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)}\bigr]+\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl[[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(j)},\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}],\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(i)}\bigr]\bigr)=(Z\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\cdot\bigl((Z\cdot\nabla)(\curl\mathbf{A})\bigr).

(4.136)

When we combine (
4.133
)-(
4.136
), this proves proves (
4.132
). We are now prepared to give the proof of (
4.125
).

We start by noting that
|A+B+C|^{2}\leqslant 3(|A|^{2}+|B|^{2}+|C|^{2})
holds for three linear operators
A,B,C
, which implies

\displaystyle(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\;\bigl(Z_{1}^{2}\;(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(1)})^{2}+Z_{2}^{2}\;(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(2)})^{2}+Z_{3}^{2}\;(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{(3)})^{2}\bigr)\leqslant 3\;|Z|^{2}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}.

(4.137)

We use (
4.127
), (
4.132
), and (
4.137
) to show

\displaystyle(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\,|Z|^{2}\,\bigl(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+2\,|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}-\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+(Z\cdot\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}))\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})+(Z\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\cdot\bigl((Z\cdot\nabla)(\curl\mathbf{A})\bigr).

Next, we write
|Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}|^{4}=(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})^{2}(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})
, apply (
4.137
) to the term in the middle, and find

\displaystyle|Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant 9\,|Z|^{4}\bigl(\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+2\,|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}-\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+3\,|Z|^{2}\bigl[(Z\cdot\curl(\curl\mathbf{A}))\,(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})+(Z\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})\cdot\bigl((Z\cdot\nabla)(\curl\mathbf{A})\bigr)\bigr].

Moreover,
AB+BA\leqslant\varepsilon\,A^{2}+\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\,B^{2}
for
\varepsilon>0
and self-adjoint operators
A
and
B
, (
4.137
), and
|Z\wedge\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}|^{2}\leqslant 3|Z|^{2}\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}
imply that the right side of the above equation is bounded by

\displaystyle C\,|Z|^{4}\bigl[\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{4}+\,|\curl\mathbf{A}|^{2}+\varepsilon\,\Pi_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}\bigl(|\curl(\curl\mathbf{A})|^{2}+|\nabla(\curl\mathbf{A})|^{2}\bigr)\bigr].

This proves part (a).

The proof of part (b) is a direct consequence of part (a) with the choice
\varepsilon=h^{2}
, and is therefore left to the reader. This proves Lemma
4.21
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.20
.

We use the definition of
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}
to write

\displaystyle\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}\Delta\rangle

\displaystyle=4\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}Z\;V\alpha_{*}(r)V\alpha_{*}(s)\,k_{T}(Z,r-s)\;\langle\Psi,\mathcal{R}(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}_{h}})\Psi\rangle.

(4.138)

The function
\mathcal{R}(x)=\cos(x)-1+\frac{x^{2}}{2}
satisfies the bound
0\leqslant\mathcal{R}(x)\leqslant\frac{1}{24}x^{4}
, and hence an application of Lemma
4.21
shows

\displaystyle\langle\Psi,\mathcal{R}(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}_{h}})\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;|Z|^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.139)

When we apply the estimate
|Z|^{4}\leqslant|Z+\frac{r}{2}|^{4}+|Z-\frac{r}{2}|^{4}
, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\;|Z|^{4}\;|k_{T}(Z,r)|

\displaystyle\leqslant F_{T}^{4}(r)

(4.140)

holds with
F_{T}^{4}
defined in (
4.93
). But this also shows

|\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{(3)}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}\;\|(V\alpha_{*})F_{T}^{4}\ast(V\alpha_{*})\|_{1}.

In combination with the
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm bound for
F_{T}^{4}
in (
4.95
), this proves the claim.
∎

4.5.5
A representation formula for the operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}

In the next five subsections we study the operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.43
). In particular, we extract the term in the GL functional that is proportional to
W
from
\langle\Delta,\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle
. The operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,0}
has previously been studied in
[
18
]
. After the magnetic field has been removed, our analysis mostly follows ideas in this reference. Because of this and because several ideas of the previous sections appear again, we keep our presentation rather short and only mention the main ideas. As in the case of
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
, we start our analysis with a representation formula for
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in terms of relative and center-of-mass coordinates.

Lemma 4.22
.

The operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\colon{L^{2}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}\rightarrow{L^{2}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
in (
4.43
) acts as

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\alpha(X,r)

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T,\mathbf{A},W}(X,Z,r,s)\;(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\alpha)(X,s),

(4.141)

where

\displaystyle k_{T,\mathbf{A},W}(X,Z,r,s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\smash{\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Y}\;W_{h}(X+Y)\bigl[k_{T,\mathbf{A},+}^{n}(X,Y,Z,r,s)\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}^{+}(Y,Z,r,s)}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.0pt+k_{T,\mathbf{A},-}^{n}(X,Y,Z,r,s)\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}^{-}(Y,Z,r,s)}\bigr],

(4.142)

where

\displaystyle k_{T,\mathbf{A},\pm}^{n}(X,Y,Z,r,s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq g_{h}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X\pm\frac{r}{2},X+Y\bigr)\;g_{h}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X+Y,X+Z\pm\frac{s}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\times g_{h}^{\mp\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X\mp\frac{r}{2},X+Z\mp\frac{s}{2}\bigr).

(4.143)

The function
g_{h}^{z}
is defined in (
4.3
) and

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\pm}(Y,Z,r,s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X\pm\frac{r}{2},X+Y\bigr)+\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X+Y,X+Z\pm\frac{s}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt+\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X\mp\frac{r}{2},X+Z\mp\frac{s}{2}\bigr).

(4.144)

Proof.

The proof that
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
is a bounded linear map on
{L^{2}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
goes along the same lines as that of Lemma
3.4
. The proof of the representation formula is analogous to the proof of Lemma
4.7
. We use (
4.40
) for
W=0
to write

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\alpha(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}w\;\bigl[G_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(x,u)\,W_{h}(u)\,G_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(u,v)\,\alpha(v,w)\,G_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(y,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt+G_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(x,v)\,\alpha(v,w)\,G_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(u,w)\,W_{h}(u)\,G_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(y,u)\bigr].

(4.145)

When we define the coordinates
X=\frac{x+y}{2}
and
r=x-y
, apply the change of variables

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=X+Y,

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=X+Z+\frac{s}{2},

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle=X+Z-\frac{s}{2},

and use (
4.3
), this yields (
4.141
). We highlight that, by a slight abuse of notation, we denoted the function
\alpha
depending on the original coordinates in (
4.145
) and the function depending on relative and center-of-mass coordinates in (
4.141
) by the same symbol.
∎

4.5.6
Approximation of the operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}

The operator
\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
will be analyzed in three steps. More precisely, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}=\bigl(\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\bigr)+\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}-\mathcal{W}_{T}\bigr)+\mathcal{W}_{T},

(4.146)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathcal{W}_{T}
are operators of increasing simplicity in their dependence on
W
and
\mathbf{A}
. They are defined below in (
4.147
) and (
4.150
), respectively. The term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional that is proportional to
W
will be extracted from the expectation of the operator
\mathcal{W}_{T}
with respect to
\Delta
. The expectation of the first two terms in (
4.146
) will be shown to be negligible.

4.5.6.1
The operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
.

We define the operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,A}\alpha(X,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{h}(X)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T,\mathbf{A},0}(X,Z,r,s)\;(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\alpha)(X,s),

(4.147)

where
k_{T,\mathbf{A},W}
is defined in (
4.142
). The following proposition allows us to estimate the expectation of the first term in (
4.146
) with respect to
\Delta
.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
|\cdot|^{k}V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, let
A\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
W\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\,h^{5}\,\max_{k=0,1}\|\,|\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\mathcal{W}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip-110.0pt\leqslant 4\,\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\;\esssup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(k_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-k_{T,\mathbf{A},0})(X,Z,r,s)|\;|V\alpha_{*}(r)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|

(4.148)

and note that

\displaystyle|(k_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-k_{T,\mathbf{A},W})(X,Z,r,s)|

\displaystyle\hskip-140.0pt\leqslant\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Y\;|W_{h}(X+Y)-W_{h}(X)|\;\bigl(|k_{T,\mathbf{A},+}^{n}|+|k_{T,\mathbf{A},-}^{n}|\bigr)(X,Y,Z,r,s).

(4.149)

When we use (
4.149
),
|W(X+Y)-W(X)|\leqslant\|\nabla W\|_{\infty}\,|Y|
,
|Y|\leqslant|Y\pm\frac{r}{2}|+|\frac{r}{2}|
, and Proposition
4.2
, we see that the right side of (
4.148
) is bounded by a constant times

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\nabla W_{h}\|_{\infty}\;\|(1+|\cdot|)(V\alpha_{*})\widetilde{F}_{T}^{1}\ast|V\alpha_{*}|\ \|_{1},

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{T}^{a}\coloneqq\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{b=0}^{a}\left[\bigl(|\cdot|^{b}\,\rho^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigr)*\rho^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}+\bigl(|\cdot|^{b}\,\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigr)*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\right].

With Proposition
4.2
and (
4.56
) we show that
\|\widetilde{F}_{T}^{a}\|_{1}\leqslant C
. In combination with the bound
\|\nabla W_{h}\|_{\infty}\leqslant Ch^{3}
, this proves the claim.
∎

4.5.6.2
The operator
\mathcal{W}_{T}
.

We define the operator
\mathcal{W}_{T}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{T}\alpha(X,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{h}(X)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T}(Z,r-s)\;\alpha(X,s)

(4.150)

where

\displaystyle k_{T}(Z,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigl(k_{T,+}^{n}(Z,r)+k_{T,-}^{n}(Z,r)\bigr)

(4.151)

and

\displaystyle k_{T,\pm}^{n}(Z,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq(g_{0}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*g_{0}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}})\bigl(Z\mp\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\;g_{0}^{\mp\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(Z\pm\frac{r}{2}\bigr).

(4.152)

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
|\cdot|^{k}V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, let
A\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
W\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\mathcal{W}_{T}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\,h^{5}\,\max_{k=0,1}\|\,|\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Sketch of proof.

The proof goes along the same lines as that of Propositions
4.9
,
4.11
, and
4.15
with the notable simplification that we only need to prove bounds for the quadratic form. We therefore only mention the main steps that need to be carried out and leave the details to the reader. In the first step
g_{h}^{z}
is replaced by
g_{0}^{z}
. In the second step the part of the magnetic phase coming from
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}
is split off. A careful analysis shows that

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}}^{\pm}(X,Y,Z,r,s)=Z\cdot(\mathbf{B}\wedge X)+\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot\theta_{\pm}(Y,Z,r,s),

where

\displaystyle\theta_{\pm}(Y,Z,r,s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\pm\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Y\mp\frac{r}{2}\bigr)+\bigl(Y\mp\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z-Y\pm\frac{s}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt\pm\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Z-Y\pm\frac{s}{2}\bigr)\mp\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Z\pm\frac{r-s}{2}\bigr).

(4.153)

The phase
\exp(\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(\mathbf{B}\wedge X))
and
\exp(\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X}))
are combined and give
\exp(\mathrm{i}Z\cdot\Pi)
, see (
4.90
). In the third step the magnetic phases coming from
\theta_{\pm}
and from the periodic vector potential
A
, that is, from
\Theta_{A}
, are removed. Afterwards, the emergent symmetry of the integrand under the transformation
Z\rightarrow-Z
is used to replace the operator
\exp(\mathrm{i}Z\cdot\Pi)
by
\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)
. In the final step, we apply the estimate
1-\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)\leqslant C|Z|^{2}\,\Pi^{2}
. This ends our sketch of proof.
∎

4.5.7
Analysis of
\mathcal{W}_{T}
and calculation of the quadratic
W
-term

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let
W\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})
be periodic, assume that
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). There is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\langle\Delta,\mathcal{W}_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}\Delta\rangle=-4\;\Lambda_{1}\;\langle\Psi,W_{h}\Psi\rangle

(4.154)

with
\Lambda_{1}
in (
3.21
). Moreover, for any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\mathcal{W}_{T}\Delta-\mathcal{W}_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;|T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}|\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.155)

Proof.

Using the definition of
k_{T}(Z,r)
in (
4.151
), we write

\displaystyle k_{T}(Z,r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}\;\Bigl[\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(p+q)}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{r}{2}\cdot(q-p)}}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\mu-p^{2})^{2}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mu+p^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt-\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(p+q)}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{r}{2}\cdot(p-q)}}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mu+p^{2})^{2}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\mu-p^{2})}\Bigr]

as well as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\;k_{T}(Z,r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{4}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}r\cdot p}\,\frac{p^{2}-\mu}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\mu-p^{2})^{2}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mu+p^{2})^{2}}.

With the Mittag-Leffler series expansion in (
4.35
), we also check that

\displaystyle\frac{\beta}{2}\frac{1}{\cosh^{2}(\frac{\beta}{2}z)}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\tanh\bigl(\frac{\beta}{2}z\bigr)=-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-z)^{2}}

(4.156)

holds. We use (
4.156
) and the partial fraction expansion

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-E)^{2}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+E)^{2}}=\frac{1}{4E^{2}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-E)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+E)^{2}}\Bigr]-\frac{1}{4E^{3}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-E}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+E}\Bigr]

to see that

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{E}{(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-E)^{2}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+E)^{2}}=\beta^{2}\;g_{1}(\beta E)

with the function
g_{1}
in (
3.19
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\Delta,M_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{W}\Delta\rangle

\displaystyle=-{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\;|(-2)\widehat{V\alpha_{*}}(p)|^{2}\,g_{1}({\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}(p^{2}-\mu))\;\langle\Psi,W_{h}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle=-4\,\Lambda_{1}\,\langle\Psi,W_{h}\Psi\rangle.

This proves (
4.154
).

To obtain the bound in (
4.155
), we argue as in the proof of (
4.124
).
∎

4.5.8
Summary: The quadratic terms

In this section, we summarize our results concerning the quadratic terms (in
\Delta
) that are relevant for the proof of Theorem
3.6
. We also use our results to prove another statement (Proposition
4.26
below), which will later be used in the proof of Proposition
3.7
. We start by summarizing our findings.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
3.6
hold and recall the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(2)}
in (
4.44
). An application of Hölder’s inequality in (
2.1
) and the bound
\|(\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leqslant|\omega_{n}|^{-1}
show that

|\langle\Delta,\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(2)}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\;\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\;\|W_{h}\|^{2}_{\infty}\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

We combine (
4.42
), this bound, and the results of Propositions
4.17
,
4.19
,
4.20
,
4.23
,
4.24
, and
4.25
, to see that for
T={T_{\mathrm{c}}}(1-Dh^{2})
with
D\in\mathbb{R}
the identity

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta\rangle+\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\,\langle\alpha_{*},V\alpha_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-80.0pt=\Lambda_{0}\;\|\Pi_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}\;\langle\Psi,W_{h}\Psi\rangle-Dh^{2}\;\Lambda_{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+R_{2}(\Delta)

(4.157)

holds. The remainder term
R_{2}(\Delta)
obeys the estimate

\displaystyle|R_{2}(\Delta)|\leqslant C\;\bigl(h^{5}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}+h^{6}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}^{2}\bigr).

This concludes the computation of the quadratic terms in the Ginzburg–Landau functional. It remains to compute the term that is proportional to
|\Psi|^{4}
, which is the content of the remaining part of Section
4.5
.

Before we continue with the proof of Theorem
3.6
, we state and prove the following statement, which will later be used in the proof of Proposition
3.7
. It is a straightforward consequence of our results for the quadratic terms, and we therefore prove it here.

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
|\cdot|^{k}V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, let
A\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
W\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta\rangle+\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\;\langle\alpha_{*},V\alpha_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant c\,\frac{T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\,\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+Ch^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.158)

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta\rangle=-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle

(4.159)

and use the resolvent identity in (
4.36
) to write one of the operators on the right side as

\displaystyle L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}W_{h}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\Delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.0pt-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\Delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}W_{h}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}}.

(4.160)

An application of Hölder’s inequality therefore implies the bound

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle|\leqslant C\,h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

When we additionally use the decomposition of
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.49
), Propositions
4.9
,
4.11
, and
4.15
, we find

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\rangle+\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\;\langle\alpha_{*},V\alpha_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-100.0pt=-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta\rangle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta\rangle+R_{1}(\Delta),

(4.161)

with a remainder
R_{1}(\Delta)
obeying the bound

\displaystyle|R_{1}(\Delta)|\leqslant C\;h^{5}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

From Proposition
4.17
we know that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta\rangle\leqslant c\;\frac{T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\;\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}.

We also claim that the bound

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}

(4.162)

holds. Its proof can be achieved with the same methods that have been used to prove Proposition
4.15
. The main point is that we have to use the bound

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,[\cos(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})-1]\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;|Z|^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2},

(4.163)

as well as the operator inequality in (
4.137
) for
(Z\cdot\Pi_{\mathbf{A}})^{2}
. Since no additional difficulties occur, we leave the details to the reader. This completes the proof of (
4.158
).
∎

4.5.9
A representation formula for the operator
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}

In this and the following sections we investigate the nonlinear operator

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{A}}\coloneqq N_{T,\mathbf{A},0}

(4.164)

with
N_{T,\mathbf{A},W}
in (
3.12
). In particular, we show that the quartic term in the GL functional emerges from
\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)\rangle
. In Section
4.5.12
we show that
\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-N_{T,\mathbf{A},W}(\Delta)\rangle
yields a negligible contribution. In this section we will also collect all previous results and finish the proof of Theorem
3.6
.

Before we state a representation formula for
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in terms of relative and center-of-mass coordinates, we introduce the notation
\mathbf{Z}
to denote the vector
(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3})
with
Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
as well as
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}=\mathrm{d}Z_{1}\mathrm{d}Z_{2}\mathrm{d}Z_{3}
.

Lemma 4.27
.

The operator
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
3.12
) acts as

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\;\mathcal{A}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s}),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{1}\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\alpha(X,s_{1})\;\overline{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{2}\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\alpha(X,s_{2})}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{3}\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\alpha(X,s_{3})

(4.165)

and

\displaystyle\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{n}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,s)}.

(4.166)

Here,

\displaystyle\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{n}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq g_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X+\frac{r}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{1}+\frac{s_{1}}{2}\bigr)\,g_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X+Z_{2}+\frac{s_{2}}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{1}-\frac{s_{1}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\times g_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X+Z_{2}-\frac{s_{2}}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{3}+\frac{s_{3}}{2}\bigr)\,g_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(X-\frac{r}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{3}-\frac{s_{3}}{2}\bigr),

(4.167)

with
g_{h}^{z}
in (
4.3
) and

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X+\frac{r}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{1}+\frac{s_{1}}{2}\bigr)+\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X+Z_{2}+\frac{s_{2}}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{1}-\frac{s_{1}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt+\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X+Z_{2}-\frac{s_{2}}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{3}+\frac{s_{3}}{2}\bigr)+\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\bigl(X-\frac{r}{2}\,,\,X+Z_{3}-\frac{s_{3}}{2}\bigr).

(4.168)

Remark 4.28
.

The above representation formula for
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
should be compared to that in the case of a constant magnetic field in
[
16
, Lemma 4.16]
and to the representation formula for
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in
4.7
. The following two properties are relevant for us: (a) The functions
\alpha
are multiplied by translation operators that can later be completed with appropriate phase factors to give magnetic translation operators. (b) The coordinates appearing in
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.168
) equal those in the different factors in the definition of
\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{n}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})
in (
4.167
). When proving bounds, this allows us to find a similar structure of nested convolutions as the one we already encountered in the analysis of
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
. The center-of-mass part of
\alpha
never participates in these convolutions.

Proof of Lemma
4.27
.

When we compute the integral kernel of
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
using (
4.40
) in the case
W=0
, we get

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\alpha)(x,y)

\displaystyle=\smash{\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}}\;G_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(x,u_{1})\,\alpha(u_{1},v_{1})\,G_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(u_{2},v_{1})\,\overline{\alpha(u_{2},v_{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\times G_{h}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(v_{2},u_{3})\,\alpha(u_{3},v_{3})\,G_{h}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}(y,v_{3}),

(4.169)

We highlight that, by a slight abuse of notation,
\alpha
and
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\alpha)
in the above equation are functions of the original coordinates, while they are functions of relative and center-of-mass coordinates in (
4.165
). Let us denote
X=\frac{x+y}{2}
,
r=x-y
and let us also introduce the relative coordinate
\mathbf{s}
and the center-of-mass coordinate
\mathbf{Z}
by

\displaystyle u_{i}

\displaystyle=X+Z_{i}+\frac{s_{i}}{2},

\displaystyle v_{i}

\displaystyle=X+Z_{i}-\frac{s_{i}}{2},

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle=1,2,3.

When we express the integration in (
4.169
) in terms of these coordinates and use (
4.3
), we see that the claimed formula holds.
∎

As the operator
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
, we analyze the operator
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in four steps. More precisely, we decompose
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
as

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{A}}=(N_{T,\mathbf{A}}-\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}})+(\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}-N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)})+(N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}-N_{T}^{(2)})+N_{T}^{(2)}

(4.170)

with
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
defined below in (
4.171
),
N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}
in (
4.180
), and
N_{T}^{(2)}
in (
4.189
). To obtain the map
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
from
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
we need to replace
g_{h}^{z}
by
g_{0}^{z}
. The operator
N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}
emerges when we use a part of the phase
\exp(\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,s))
in the definition of
\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.166
) to replace the translation operators
\exp(\mathrm{i}Z\cdot P_{X})
in front the
\alpha
factors by magnetic translation operators. The part of the phase factor that is not needed during this procedure is shown to yield a negligible contribution. Finally, the operator
N_{T}^{(2)}
is obtained when we replace the just found magnetic translations by
1
. The above decomposition of
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
should be compared to that in
[
16
, Eq. (4.120)]
. In Section
4.5.10
we show that the terms in brackets in (
4.170
) only yield negligible contributions. Afterwards, we extract in Section
4.5.11
the quartic term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional from
\langle\Delta,N_{T}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle
. In Section
4.5.12
we summarize our findings.

4.5.10
Approximation of
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}

4.5.10.1
The operator
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
.

We define the operator
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;\widetilde{\ell}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\;\mathcal{A}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s})

(4.171)

with
\mathcal{A}
in (
4.165
) and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ell}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\ell_{T}^{n}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})},

where
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}}
has been defined in (
4.168
), and

\displaystyle\ell_{T}^{n}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(Z_{1}-\frac{r-s_{1}}{2}\bigr)\;g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(Z_{1}-Z_{2}-\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt\times g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(Z_{2}-Z_{3}-\frac{s_{2}+s_{3}}{2}\bigr)\;g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}\bigl(Z_{3}+\frac{r-s_{3}}{2}\bigr).

(4.172)

In our calculation of the BCS energy we can replace
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)
by
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)
because of the following error bound.

Proposition 4.29
.

Let
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let
A\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume that
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}.

The function

\displaystyle J_{T,\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\smash{\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}}\;\tau^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\tau^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\tau^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}*\rho^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\tau^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}.

(4.173)

plays a prominent role in the proof of Proposition
4.29
. Using Lemmas
4.2
and
4.4
as well as (
4.56
), we see that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|J_{T,\mathbf{A}_{h}}\|_{1}\leqslant C\;h^{3}

(4.174)

holds.

Proof of Proposition
4.29
.

The function
|\Psi|
is periodic, and hence (
2.9
) implies

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\Psi\|_{6}^{2}=\|\Psi\|_{6}^{2}\leqslant C\,h^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.175)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\fint_{Q_{h}}\mathrm{d}X\;|\Psi(X)|\;\prod_{i=1}^{3}|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{i}\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\Psi(X)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Psi\|_{2}\;\prod_{i=1}^{3}\|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{i}\cdot(-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{X})}\Psi\|_{6}\leqslant C\,h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}

(4.176)

as well as

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip-100.0pt\leqslant C\,h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h}))}^{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;|V\alpha_{*}(r)|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{1})|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{2})|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{3})|

\displaystyle\hskip-20.0pt\times\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\;\esssup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|(\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}-\widetilde{\ell}_{T,\mathbf{A}})(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\bigr|.

(4.177)

Next, we define the variables
Z_{1}^{\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime},Z_{3}^{\prime}
via the equation

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq Z_{1}-Z_{2}-\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{2},

\displaystyle Z_{2}^{\prime}-Z_{3}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq Z_{2}-Z_{3}-\frac{s_{2}+s_{3}}{2},

\displaystyle Z_{3}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq Z_{3}+\frac{r-s_{3}}{2},

(4.178)

which implies

\displaystyle Z_{1}-\frac{r-s_{1}}{2}=Z_{1}^{\prime}-(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3}).

(4.179)

We argue as in the proof of (
4.65
) to see that

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\;\esssup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|(\ell_{T,\mathbf{A}}-\widetilde{\ell}_{T,\mathbf{A}})(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\bigr|\leqslant J_{T,\mathbf{A}_{h}}(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3})

holds with
J_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.173
). When insert this bound into (
4.177
) and use

\displaystyle\bigl\|V\alpha_{*}\;\bigl(V\alpha_{*}*V\alpha_{*}*V\alpha_{*}*J_{T,\mathbf{A}}\bigr)\bigr\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\;\|J_{T,\mathbf{A}}\|_{1},

as well as (
4.174
), this finishes the proof.
∎

4.5.10.2
The operator
N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}
.

We define the operator
N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}
by

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;\ell_{T}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\;\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{B}}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s}),

(4.180)

where

\displaystyle\ell_{T}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ell_{T,0}(0,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s}),

(4.181)

with
\ell_{T,0}
in (
4.166
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{B}}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{1}\cdot\Pi}\alpha(X,s_{1})\;\overline{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{2}\cdot\Pi}\alpha(X,s_{2})}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z_{3}\cdot\Pi}\alpha(X,s_{3}).

(4.182)

The following bound allows us to replace
\langle\Delta,\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)\rangle
by
\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)\rangle
in our computation of the energy.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
|\cdot|V\alpha_{*}\in L^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, let
A\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{5}\;\max_{k=0,1}\|\ |\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}.

Before we give the proof of Proposition
4.30
we define the functions

\displaystyle J_{T}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\smash{\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}}\,|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)

(4.183)

and

\displaystyle J_{T}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\smash{\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}}\,\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr)*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|+|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*|g_{0}^{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|*\bigl(|\cdot|\,|g_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}}|\bigr).

(4.184)

Using Lemma
4.4
and (
4.56
), we show that for any
T_{0}>0
there is a constant
C>0
such that for
T\geqslant T_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\|J_{T}^{(1)}\|_{1}+\|J_{T}^{(2)}\|_{1}\leqslant C.

(4.185)

Proof of Proposition
4.30
.

We recall the definition of the phase
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.168
). A tedious but straightforward computation shows that

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=Z_{1}\cdot(\mathbf{B}\wedge X)-Z_{2}\cdot(\mathbf{B}\wedge X)+Z_{3}\cdot(\mathbf{B}\wedge X)+\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s}),

where

\displaystyle I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Z_{1}-\frac{r-s_{1}}{2}\bigr)+\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Z_{3}+\frac{r-s_{3}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-35.0pt+\bigl(Z_{2}-Z_{3}-\frac{s_{2}+s_{3}}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z_{1}-Z_{2}-\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-35.0pt+\bigl(Z_{3}+\frac{r-s_{3}}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z_{1}-Z_{2}-\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{2}\bigr)+\bigl(s_{2}+s_{3}-\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z_{1}-Z_{2}-\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-35.0pt+\bigl(Z_{3}+\frac{r-s_{3}}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z_{3}-Z_{2}+\frac{s_{2}+s_{3}}{2}\bigr)+\bigl(s_{3}-\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(Z_{3}-Z_{2}+\frac{s_{2}+s_{3}}{2}\bigr).

(4.186)

By (
4.90
), the operator
\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}
can therefore be rewritten as

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}s\;\ell_{T}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{A_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}\;\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{B}}(X,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{s}).

This formula and the estimate in (
4.176
) imply the bound

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip-120.0pt\leqslant C\;h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;|V\alpha_{*}(r)|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{1})|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{2})|\;|V\alpha_{*}(s_{3})|

\displaystyle\hskip-100.0pt\times\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\;|\ell_{T}^{n}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})|\;\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{A_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}-1\bigr|

(4.187)

with
\Upsilon_{A}
in (
4.168
) and
I
in (
4.186
). In terms of the coordinates in (
4.178
) and with the help of (
4.179
), the phase function
I
can be written as

\displaystyle I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=(Z_{2}^{\prime}-Z_{3}^{\prime})\wedge(Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})+Z_{3}^{\prime}\wedge(Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})+Z_{3}^{\prime}\wedge(Z_{3}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\frac{r}{2}\wedge\bigl(Z_{1}^{\prime}-(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3})\bigr)+\bigl(s_{2}+s_{3}-\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\wedge(Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\bigl(s_{3}-\frac{r}{2}\bigr)\wedge(Z_{3}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})+\frac{r}{2}\wedge Z_{3}^{\prime}.

(4.188)

Moreover, using the definition of
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.2
), the definition of
\Upsilon_{A}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})
in (
4.168
), (
4.178
), and (
4.179
), we obtain the bound

\displaystyle|\Upsilon_{A}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A\|_{\infty}\bigl((Z_{1}^{\prime}-(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3}))+(Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime})+(Z_{2}^{\prime}-Z_{3}^{\prime})+Z_{3}^{\prime}\bigr).

In combination with (
4.187
), (
4.188
), and an argument that is similar to the one used to obtain (
4.114
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\;|\ell_{T,0}^{n}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})|\;\bigl|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{A_{h}}(X,\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot I(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})}-1\bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\leqslant Ch\;\bigl[J_{T}^{(1)}(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3})+J_{T}^{(2)}(r-s_{1}-s_{2}-s_{3})\;\bigl(1+|r|+|s_{1}|+|s_{2}|+|s_{3}|\bigr)\bigr]

with the functions
F_{T}^{(1)}
and
F_{T}^{(2)}
in (
4.183
) and (
4.184
), respectively. Accordingly, an application of Young’s inequality shows that

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,\widetilde{N}_{T,\mathbf{A}}(\Delta)-N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip-100.0pt\leqslant C\;h^{5}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}\;\bigl(\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}+\|\,|\cdot|V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\bigr)\bigl(\|J_{T}^{(1)}\|_{1}+\|J_{T}^{(2)}\|_{1}\bigr).

The claim of the proposition follows when we apply (
4.185
) on the right side of the above equation.
∎

4.5.10.3
The operator
N_{T}^{(2)}
.

We define the operator
N_{T}^{(2)}
by

\displaystyle N_{T}^{(2)}(\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\;\ell_{T}(\mathbf{Z},r,\mathbf{s})\,\prod_{i=1}^{3}\alpha(X,s_{i})

(4.189)

with
\ell_{T}
in (
4.181
).

In the computation of the BCS energy we can replace
\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)\rangle
by
\langle\Delta,N_{T}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle
with the help of the following error bound. Its proof can be found in
[
16
, Proposition 4.20]
. We highlight that the
H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
-norm of
\Psi
is needed once more.

Proposition 4.31
.

Assume that
|\cdot|^{k}V\alpha_{*}\in L^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, let
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
, and
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{B}}^{(1)}(\Delta)-N_{T}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\max_{k=0,1,2}\|\ |\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{3}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}.

4.5.11
Calculation of the quartic term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional

The quartic term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional in (
1.18
) is contained in
\langle\Delta,N_{T}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle
. It can be extracted with the following proposition, whose proof can be found in
[
16
, Proposition 4.21]
.

Proposition 4.32
.

Assume
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and let
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
as well as
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
h>0
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\Delta,N_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle=8\;\Lambda_{3}\;\|\Psi\|_{4}^{4}

with
\Lambda_{3}
in (
3.23
). Moreover, for any
T\geqslant T_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta,N_{T}^{(2)}(\Delta)-N_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(2)}(\Delta)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;h^{4}\;|T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}|\;\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{4}.

4.5.12
Summary: The quartic term and proof of Theorem
3.6

Let the assumptions of Theorem
3.6
hold. We use the resolvent identity in (
4.36
) to decompose the operator
N_{T,\mathbf{A},W}
in (
3.12
) as

\displaystyle N_{T,\mathbf{A},W}=N_{T,\mathbf{A}}+\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(3)}

(4.190)

with
N_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.164
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(3)}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\coloneqq\frac{2}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,W_{h}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}\,\overline{\Delta}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}}\,W_{h}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}\,\overline{\Delta}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}}\,\overline{\Delta}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,W_{h}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}}\,\overline{\Delta}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A}}}}\,W_{h}\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}\Bigr].

(4.191)

We claim that the operator
\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(3)}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(3)}(\Delta)\|_{{L^{2}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,T^{-5}\,h^{5}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{3}.

This is a direct consequence of Hölder’s inequality in (
2.1
) for the trace per unit volume, which implies that the Hilbert-Schmidt norm per unit volume of the terms in the sum in (
4.191
) are bounded by
C\,|\omega_{n}|^{-5}\,\|W_{h}\|_{\infty}\,\|\Delta\|_{6}^{3}
. Moreover, an application of Lemma
4.1
and (
2.9
) show that this expression is bounded by
C\,|2n+1|^{-5}\,T^{-5}\,h^{5}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{3}
, which proves our claim.

When we combine this bound, Lemma
4.27
, and Propositions
4.29
-
4.32
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\langle\Delta,N_{T,\mathbf{A},W}(\Delta)\rangle=\;\Lambda_{3}\;\|\Psi\|_{4}^{4}+R_{4}(h),

(4.192)

where the remainder
R(h)
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle|R_{4}(h)|\leqslant C\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{3}\,\bigl(h^{5}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}+h^{6}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}\bigr).

The statement in Theorem
3.6
is a direct consequence of (
4.192
) and (
4.157
).

4.6
Proof of Proposition
3.2

We assume that the assumptions of Proposition
3.2
hold and recall the definition of
\Gamma_{\Delta}
in (
3.4
). Using the resolvent equation in (
4.36
) and (
4.37
), we write
\alpha_{\Delta}=[\Gamma_{\Delta}]_{12}
as

\displaystyle\alpha_{\Delta}

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{O}]_{12}+\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta).

Here,
\mathcal{O}=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{0}}\delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{0}}
, see (
4.38
), with
\delta
in (
3.1
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{0}}\delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{0}}\delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{\Delta}}\delta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{0}}\Bigr]_{12}.

(4.193)

Moreover, we have
[\mathcal{O}]_{12}=-\frac{1}{2}L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta
with
L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}
in (
3.11
). Using the decomposition of
L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}
in (
4.42
), we define

\displaystyle\eta_{0}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\bigl(L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta-M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta\bigr)+\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta),

\displaystyle\eta_{\perp}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\bigl(M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta-M_{T}^{(1)}\Delta\bigr),

(4.194)

with
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.108
),
L_{T,\mathbf{A}}^{W}
in (
4.43
), and
M_{T}^{(1)}
in (
4.120
). From Proposition
4.17
we know that
-\frac{1}{2}M_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}^{(1)}\Delta=\Psi\alpha_{*}
, which allows us to write
\alpha_{\Delta}
as in (
3.6
). The operator
M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}
equals
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}
in (
4.108
) with
\mathbf{A}
replaced by
\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}
. The contribution from this operator needs to be carefully isolated for the orthogonality property in (
3.9
) to hold. This should be compared to part (c) of
[
16
, Proposition 3.2]
. In the following, we will establish the properties of
\eta_{0}
and
\eta_{\perp}
that are stated in Proposition
3.2
.

We will first prove (
3.7
), and start by noting that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}\,\overline{\Delta}\,\Bigl[\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{\Delta}}\Bigr]_{11}\,\Delta\,\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}.

An application of Hölder’s inequality shows
\|\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)\|_{2}\leqslant C\beta^{3}\|\Delta\|_{6}^{3}
. With the operator
\pi=-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}
understood to act on the
x
-coordinate of the integral kernel of
\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)
we also have

\|\pi\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)\|_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigl\|\pi\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\overline{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}^{2}\Bigl\|\Bigl[\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-H_{\Delta}}\Bigr]_{11}\Bigr\|_{\infty}\|\Delta\|_{6}^{3}.

An application of Cauchy–Schwarz shows

(-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}+A)^{2}+W_{h}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}(-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}})^{2}-Ch^{2}.

(4.195)

Accordingly, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\pi\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\pi^{2}\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}+\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}+\mu+Ch^{2}\right)\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\omega_{n}-\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{A},W}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leqslant C\,|\omega_{n}|^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|\pi\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)\|_{2}\leqslant C\,\|\Delta\|_{6}^{3}.

(4.196)

The same argument with obvious adjustments also shows that
\|\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)\pi\|_{2}
is bounded by the right side of (
4.196
), too. Finally, (
2.13
), an application of Lemma
4.1
, and (
2.9
) allow us to conclude that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{T,\mathbf{A},W}^{(4)}(\Delta)\|_{{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}}^{2}\leqslant C\;h^{6}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{6}

(4.197)

holds.

To control
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta
, we need the following proposition.

Proposition 4.33
.

Let
|\cdot|V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, let
A\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\|M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta\|_{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,h^{5}\,\max_{k=0,1}\|\ |\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(4.198)

Proof.

Let us define the operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\alpha(X,r)\coloneqq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T}(Z,r-s)\;\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A_{h}(X)\cdot Z}\;(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot\Pi}\alpha)(X,s),

where
k_{T}(Z,r)\coloneqq k_{T,0}(0,Z,r,0)
with
k_{T,0}
in (
4.45
) and
\Pi=-\mathrm{i}\nabla+2\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}
is understood to act on the center-of-mass coordinate
X
of
\alpha
. We start our analysis by writing

\displaystyle M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta=\bigl(M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta\bigr)+\bigl(Q_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta\bigr).

(4.199)

In the following we derive bounds on the
{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
-norms of the two terms on the right side of (
4.198
). When we use that the integrand in the definition of
M_{T,\mathbf{A}}
is symmetric with respect to the transformation
Z\mapsto-Z
and apply Lemma
4.13
, we see that

\displaystyle(M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\alpha-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle\hskip-100.0pt=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T}(Z,r-s)\,\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A_{h}}(X,X+Z)}-\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A_{h}(X)\cdot Z}\bigr]\,(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot\Pi}\alpha)(X,s)

holds. Let us also recall that
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
is defined in (
4.2
).

We have

\displaystyle\Phi_{2A}(X,X+Z)-2A(X)\cdot Z=2\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\;\bigl[A(X+(1-t)Z)-A(X)\bigr]\cdot Z,

and hence

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A}(X,X+Z)}-\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A(X)\cdot Z}\bigr|\leqslant\|DA\|_{\infty}\;|Z|^{2}.

(4.200)

When we apply this bound and
|Z|^{2}\leqslant|Z+\frac{r}{2}|^{2}+|Z-\frac{r}{2}|^{2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\,\|DA_{h}\|_{\infty}^{2}\,\|F_{T}^{2}\|_{1}\,\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}

with
F_{T}^{2}
in (
4.93
). Using the
L^{1}
-norm bound for
F_{T}^{2}
in (
4.95
), we conclude the claimed estimate for this term.

To obtain a bound for the first gradient term, we start by noting that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(M_{T,A}\Delta-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\Bigl|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;|k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|\nabla_{X}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A_{h}}(X,X+Z)}-\nabla_{X}\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A_{h}(X)\cdot Z}\bigr|\,\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\,\|\Pi\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}Z\cdot\Pi}\Psi\|_{2}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\Bigl|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;|k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A_{h}}(X,X+Z)}-\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A_{h}(X)\cdot Z}\bigr|\,\bigr|^{2},

where
\Pi=-\mathrm{i}\nabla+2\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}
is understood to act on the center-of-mass coordinate. When we additionally use

\displaystyle\bigl|\nabla_{X}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A}(X,X+Z)}-\nabla_{X}\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A(X)\cdot Z}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl|\nabla_{X}\Phi_{2A}(X,X+Z)-2\nabla_{X}A(X)\cdot Z\bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip-20.0pt+\bigl|\Phi_{2A}(X,X+Z)-2A(X)\cdot Z\bigr|\,|\nabla_{X}A(X)\cdot Z|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl[\|D^{2}A\|_{\infty}+\|DA\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigr]\bigl[|Z|^{2}+|Z|^{3}\bigr],

as well as

|Z|^{a}\leqslant\left|Z+\frac{r}{2}\right|^{a}+\left|Z-\frac{r}{2}\right|^{a},\quad a\geqslant 0

(4.201)

for the choices
a=2,3
,
\|D^{2}A_{h}\|_{\infty}\leqslant Ch^{3}
,
\|DA_{h}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leqslant Ch^{4}
, (
4.117
), and (
4.200
), we see that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,h^{5}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}\,\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\left(\|F_{T}^{2}\|_{1}^{2}+\|F_{T}^{3}\|_{1}^{2}\right)

holds with
F_{T}^{a}
in (
4.95
). An application of (
4.95
) proves the claimed bound for this term.

In the last step we consider

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\pi}(M_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta-\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-130.0pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\Bigl|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;|\widetilde{\pi}k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|\,\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigl|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi_{2A_{h}}(X,X+Z)}-\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}A_{h}(X)\cdot Z}\bigr|\Bigr|^{2}.

The estimate in (
4.201
) and
\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{B}\wedge r|\leqslant\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{B}|(|r-s|+|s|)
allow us to prove the bound

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\;|\widetilde{\pi}k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|Z|^{2}\leqslant F_{T}^{3}(r-s)\,(1+|s|)+G_{T}^{2}(r-s)

(4.202)

with
F_{T}^{a}
in (
4.93
) and
G_{T}^{a}
in (
4.94
). In combination with (
4.200
), this proves the claimed bound for this term. It also ends the proof of the claimed bound for the first term on the right side of (
4.199
). It remains to consider the second term.

A short computation that uses
k_{T}(-Z,r-s)=k_{T}(Z,r-s)
and
\cos(x)-1=-2\sin^{2}(\frac{x}{2})
shows

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\alpha-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\alpha)(X,r)

\displaystyle\hskip-60.0pt=-2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T}(Z,r-s)\,\sin^{2}(A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)(\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)\alpha)(X,s)

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;k_{T}(Z,r-s)\,\sin(2A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)\,(\sin(Z\cdot\Pi)\alpha)(X,s).

From this, we check that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\bigl[\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\,\|A_{h}\|_{\infty}^{4}+\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\|A_{h}\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigr]\,\|F_{T}^{2}\|_{1}^{2}\,\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}

holds with
F_{T}^{a}
in (
4.93
). When we use (
4.200
) to obtain a bound for the
L^{1}
-norm of
F_{T}^{2}
, this proves the claimed bound for this term.

Next, we note that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta)\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\Bigl|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;|k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|

\displaystyle\times\Bigl[\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla_{X}\sin^{2}(A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)|\,\|\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}+\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sin^{2}(A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)|\,\|\Pi\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sin(2A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)|\,\|\sin(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}+\sup_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sin(2A_{h}(X)\cdot Z)|\,\|\Pi\sin(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}\Bigr]\Bigr|^{2}.

(4.203)

We have

\displaystyle\|\sin(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}\leqslant C\,h^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}\,|Z|.

Furthermore, from a straight forward computation or from
[
16
, Lemma 5.12]
, we know that

\displaystyle\Pi_{X}\,\sin(Z\cdot\Pi_{X})=\sin(Z\cdot\Pi_{X})\;\Pi_{X}+2\mathrm{i}\,\cos(Z\cdot\Pi_{X})\;\mathbf{B}\wedge Z,

and hence

\displaystyle\|\Pi\sin(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,h^{2}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}\,(1+|Z|).

To obtain the bound we used that
|\mathbf{B}|=h^{2}
. For
\Pi\cos(Z\cdot\Pi)\Psi
a similar estimate was obtained in (
4.116
). Putting these bounds together, we find that the term on the left side of (
4.203
) is bounded by a constant times
h^{6}\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}
. It remains to consider the term proportional to
\widetilde{\pi}
.

A straightforward computation shows that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\pi}(\mathcal{Q}_{T,\mathbf{B},A}\Delta-M_{T,\mathbf{A}_{e_{3}}}\Delta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigl[\|A_{h}\|_{\infty}^{4}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\|A_{h}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\Bigl|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}Z\mathrm{d}s\;|\widetilde{\pi}k_{T}(Z,r-s)|\,|V\alpha_{*}(s)|\,|Z|^{2}\Bigr|^{2}.

We use (
4.202
) to see that the term on the left side is bounded by a constant times
h^{6}\max_{k=0,1}\|\ |\cdot|^{k}\ V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}
. This proves Proposition
4.33
.
∎

The next lemma provides us with a bound for the term in (
4.194
) that is proportional to
L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}
.

Lemma 4.34
.

Let
V\alpha_{*}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let
W\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
A\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be periodic, assume
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
, and denote
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi}
as in (
3.1
). For any
T_{0}>0
there is
h_{0}>0
such that for any
T\geqslant T_{0}
and any
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\|L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta\|_{{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,h^{6}\,\|V\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

Proof.

To prove the lemma, we write
L_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\Delta-L_{T,\mathbf{A}}\Delta
as in (
4.160
) and use the representation of the
H^{1}
-norm in (
2.13
). The details are a straight forward application of arguments that have been used already several times above, and are therefore left to the reader.
∎

When we combine Propositions
4.9
,
4.11
,
4.15
,
4.17
as well as (
4.197
) and Lemma
4.34
, we obtain the claimed bound for
\eta_{0}(\Delta)
in (
3.7
), that is, part (a) of Proposition
3.2
. The proofs of parts (b) and (c) can be found in
[
16
]
, see the proofs of Proposition 3.2 (b), (c). This ends our proof of Proposition
3.2
.

4.7
Proof of Proposition
3.7

Let the assumptions of Proposition
3.7
hold. In the following we prove that there are constants
D_{0}>0
and
h_{0}>0
such that for
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
and

\displaystyle 0<T_{0}\leqslant T<{T_{\mathrm{c}}}(1-D_{0}h^{2})

there is a function
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
, such that the energy of the Gibbs state
\Gamma_{\Delta}
in (
3.4
) with gap function
\Delta(X,r)=-2V\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi(X)
satisfies (
3.24
).

Let
\psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{1})
with
\|\psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}=1
and define
\Psi(X)=h\psi(hX)
. The function
\Psi
satisfies
\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})}=1
. When we apply Propositions
3.2
,
3.5
,
4.26
, as well as (
2.9
) and (
4.192
), we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma_{\Delta})-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma_{0})

\displaystyle<h^{2}\,\bigl(-cD_{0}\,\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}+C\bigr)

for
h
small enough.
The proof of Proposition
3.7
is completed when we choose
D_{0}=\frac{C}{c\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}}
.

5
The Structure of Low-Energy States

In this section we prove a priori bounds for low-energy states of the BCS functional in the sense of (
5.1
) below. The goal is to show that their Cooper pair wave function has a structure similar to that of the trial state we use in the proof of the upper bound in Section
3
. These bounds and the trial state analysis in Section
3
are the main technical ingredients for the proof of the lower bound in Section
6
. To prove the a priori bounds, we show that the periodic external potentials
W_{h}
and
A_{h}
can be treated as a perturbation, which reduces the problem to proving a priori bounds for the case of a constant magnetic field. The solution of this problem has been the main novelty in
[
16
, Theorem 5.1]
and we apply it here. In case of a magnetic field with zero flux through the unit cell such bounds have been proved for the first time in
[
21
]
. The idea to reduce the problem to the case of a constant magnetic field is inspired by a similar perturbative analysis in
[
21
]
.

We recall the definition of the generalized one-particle density matrix
\Gamma
in (
1.5
), its Cooper pair wave function
\alpha=\Gamma_{12}
, as well as the normal state
\Gamma_{0}
in (
1.12
).

Theorem 5.1

(Structure of low-energy states)
.

Let Assumptions
1.1
and
1.2
hold. For given
D_{0},D_{1}\geqslant 0
, there is a constant
h_{0}>0
such that for all
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
the following holds: If
T>0
obeys
T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}\geqslant-D_{0}h^{2}
and if
\Gamma
is a gauge-periodic state with low energy, that is,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma_{0})\leqslant D_{1}h^{4},

(5.1)

then there are
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
and
\xi\in{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
such that

\displaystyle\alpha(X,r)=\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi(X)+\xi(X,r),

(5.2)

where

\displaystyle\sup_{0<h\leqslant h_{0}}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C,

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{H^{1}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{4}\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr).

(5.3)

Remarks
.

(a)

Equation (
5.3
) shows that
\Psi
is a macroscopic quantity in the sense that its
H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
-norm scales as that of the function in (
1.20
). It is important to note that the
H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})
-norm is scaled with
h
, see (
2.7
). The unscaled
L_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
-norm of
\Psi
is of the order
h
, and therefore much larger than that of
\xi
, see (
5.3
).

(b)

Theorem
5.1
has been proven in
[
16
, Theorem 5.1]
for the case of a constant external magnetic field, where
A_{h}=0
and
W_{h}=0
. Our proof of Theorem
5.1
for general external fields reduces the problem to that case.

Although Theorem
5.1
contains the natural a priori bounds for low-energy states, we need a slightly different version of it in our proof of the lower bound for the BCS free energy in Section
6
. The main reason is that we intend to use the function
\Psi
from the decomposition of the Cooper pair wave function of a low-energy state in (
5.2
) to construct a Gibbs state
\Gamma_{\Delta}
as in (
3.4
). In order to be able to justify the relevant computations with this state, we need
\Psi\in H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
, which is not guaranteed by Theorem
5.1
above, see also Remark
3.3
. The following corollary provides us with a decomposition of
\alpha
, where the center-of-mass wave function
\Psi_{\leqslant}
has the required
H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{h})
-regularity. A decomposition with a cut-off function of the form in the corollary has also been used in
[
21
,
22
,
19
,
16
]
.

Corollary 5.2
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
5.1
hold and let
\varepsilon\in[h^{2},h_{0}^{2}]
. Let
\Psi
be as in
(
5.2
) and define

\displaystyle\Psi_{\leqslant}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbbs 1_{[0,\varepsilon]}(\Pi^{2})\Psi,

\displaystyle\Psi_{>}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbbs 1_{(\varepsilon,\infty)}(\Pi^{2})\Psi.

(5.4)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{k}(Q_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,(\varepsilon h^{-2})^{k-1}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2},\qquad k\geqslant 2,

(5.5)

as well as

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-1}h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\Pi\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}.

(5.6)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}(X,r)\coloneqq\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi_{>}(X)

(5.7)

satisfies

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{0}\|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{symm}}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-1}h^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}

(5.8)

and, with
\xi
in (
5.2
), the function

\displaystyle\sigma\coloneqq\xi+\sigma_{0}

(5.9)

obeys

\displaystyle\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{symm}}(Q_{h}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\leqslant Ch^{4}\bigl(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{h})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr).

(5.10)

In terms of these functions, the Cooper pair wave function
\alpha
of the low-energy state
\Gamma
in (
5.1
) admits the decomposition

\displaystyle\alpha(X,r)=\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi_{\leqslant}(X)+\sigma(X,r).

(5.11)

For a proof of the corollary we refer to the proof of Corollary 5.2 in
[
16
]
.

5.1
A lower bound for the BCS functional

We start the proof of Theorem
5.1
with the following lower bound on the BCS functional, whose proof is literally the same as that of the comparable statement in
[
21
]
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Gamma_{0}
be the normal state in (
1.12
). We have the lower bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma_{0})\geqslant\Tr\bigl[(K_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-V)\alpha\alpha^{*}\bigr]+\frac{4T}{5}\Tr\bigl[(\alpha^{*}\alpha)^{2}\bigr],

(5.12)

where

\displaystyle K_{T,\mathbf{A},W}=\frac{(-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{h})^{2}+W_{h}-\mu}{\tanh(\frac{(-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{h})^{2}+W_{h}-\mu}{2T})}

(5.13)

and
V\alpha(x,y)=V(x-y)\alpha(x,y)
.

In Proposition
A.1
in Appendix
A
we show that the external electric and magnetic fields can lower the lowest eigenvalue zero of
K_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}-V
at most by a constant times
h^{2}
. We use this in the next lemma to show that
K_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-V
is bounded from below by a nonnegative operator, up to a correction of the size
Ch^{2}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let Assumptions
1.1
and
1.2
be true. For any
D_{0}\geqslant 0
, there are constants
h_{0}>0
and
T_{0}>0
such that for
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
and
T>0
with
T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}}\geqslant-D_{0}h^{2}
, the estimate

\displaystyle K_{T,\mathbf{A},W}-V

\displaystyle\geqslant c\;(1-P)(1+\pi^{2})(1-P)+c\,\min\{T_{0},(T-{T_{\mathrm{c}}})_{+}\}-Ch^{2}

(5.14)

holds. Here,
P=|\alpha_{*}\rangle\langle\alpha_{*}|
is the orthogonal projection onto the ground state
\alpha_{*}
of
K_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}-V
and
\pi=-\mathrm{i}\nabla+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{B}}
.

Proof.

Since
W\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
we can use Lemma
A.7
to show that
K_{T,\mathbf{A},W}\geqslant K_{T,\mathbf{A},0}-Ch^{2}
holds. The rest of the proof goes along the same lines as that of
[
16
, Lemma 5.4]
with the obvious replacements. In particular,
[
16
, Proposition A.1]
needs to be replaced by Proposition
A.1
. We omit the details.
∎

We deduce two corollaries from (
5.12
) and Lemma
5.4
. The first statement is an a priori bound that will be used in the proof of Theorem
2
(b). Its proof goes along the same lines as that of
[
16
, Corollary 5.5]
.

Corollary 5.5
.

Let Assumptions
1.1
and
1.2
be true. Then, there are constants
h_{0}>0
and
C>0
such that for all
0<h\leqslant h_{0}
and all temperatures
T\geqslant{T_{\mathrm{c}}}(1+Ch^{2})
, we have
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{h,T}(\Gamma_{0})>0
unless
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0}
.

The second corollary provides us with an inequality for Cooper pair wave functions of low-energy BCS states in the sense of (
5.1
). The left side of (
5.16
